## ⭐ Compendium 2.0 success criterion (load-bearing)

The deliverable is a **populated data layer** — N canonical-question rows × 50 states × M vintages × {legal_availability, practical_availability} cells. The architecture commits to:

1. **ONE compendium.** A single canonical row set; no rubric-specific compendium variants.
2. **ONE extraction pipeline.** A single extraction methodology — same prompt structure, same model, same retrieval approach — applied uniformly across rows, states, and years. Not 9 different pipelines per rubric. The compendium row schema must be uniform enough that one extraction approach works for every row.
3. **Multi-year reliability.** The pipeline must extract reliably across vintages (e.g., OH 2010 + OH 2015 + OH 2024 + OH 2025), not just at one vintage.
4. **Per-rubric projections are SANITY CHECKS on extraction accuracy, not the goal.** Each source rubric (PRI 2010, CPI 2015, FOCAL 2024, Sunlight 2015, Newmark2005/2017, Opheim, HiredGuns, OpenSecrets) has a small projection function `f_rubric(compendium_cells_for_state_year) → rubric_score`. The published rubric per-state scores are **independent ground-truth yardsticks** against our extraction.

**Multi-rubric × multi-year validation = redundant per-row ground truth.** The same compendium row gets validated by every rubric that reads it (e.g., "expenditures benefitting officials" is read by Newmark2005 + Newmark2017 + Opheim + FOCAL.financials.10 — that's 4 independent checks on that one row's extraction). Different rubric vintages (PRI 2010, Sunlight 2015, CPI 2015, FOCAL 2024) validate different extraction vintages.

**Falsifiable test:** populate compendium for state S in vintage Y via the single extraction pipeline → apply each rubric's projection → compare each projected per-state score to the rubric's published score for (S, Y). Match within tolerance ⇒ extraction is sound on the rows that rubric reads, in vintage Y. **All rubrics must pass for the vintages they cover, on a sample of states.**

**Goal: minimum compendium where all rubrics still project correctly across all vintages.** Rows used by no rubric's projection are deletion candidates; rubrics that fail to project force missing rows in. Rows that pass on some rubrics but fail on others surface either a row-design issue or an extraction-pipeline issue — both are debuggable signals.

If we have a single compendium, with a single extraction prompt, that extracts reliably across multiple years, and every row is validated against multiple prior human-generated rubrics — we're in great shape.

This criterion was articulated 2026-05-07 after the 3-way × 3-run consensus pass and the CPI 2015 C11 fold-in. It sharpens the 2026-04-29 reframe ("compendium is the universe, rubrics are projections of it"). See `docs/active/compendium-source-extracts/RESEARCH_LOG.md` 2026-05-07 entry and the compare/contrast at `docs/active/compendium-source-extracts/results/20260507_cpi_2015_c11_vs_consensus.md`.

## Current Focus

**2026-05-24 update — Prong 1 paused; gather-first pivot ahead of v2.2 compendium.** First-state pilot on `extraction-harness-brainstorm` (OH 2025, Tier-1 + Tier-2) produced empirical evidence that frontier LLMs *can* read state lobbying statutes (high cross-vintage stability, low cost, no hallucinated citations) but the v2.1 typed-cell schema *cannot yet faithfully represent what statutes actually say* — most visibly on qualitative-trigger states like OH where the numeric `threshold_*` cells and `TimeThresholdCell` force a lossy encoding for the "main purpose" lobbyist gate (Tier-2 Step D writeup: "model right, schema can't represent the answer"). All three in-flight Prong 1 branches — `phase-c-projection-tdd` (5 of 8 rubric projections + 2 of 4 FOCAL plans shipped; HG + Opheim deferred on vintage), `extraction-harness-brainstorm` (Tier-0 + Tier-1 + Tier-2 harness), `oh-statute-retrieval` (stub) — merged to main and archived to `docs/historical/`. **Pivot: gather-first.** Collect per-(state, vintage, question) answers in a flexible intermediate JSON (freeform answer + statute citation + confidence) across all priority states, then design the **v2.2** typed schema from observed reality. v2.1 (current frozen 181-row TSV with column-rename refinements) remains the immediate reference. See README "Research question" + `docs/RESEARCH_ARC.md` "Status" header. Prong 2 (portal extraction) + Prong 3 (display) are unaffected; product focus continues there.

**Architectural pivot landed 2026-04-22:** project identity is **data infrastructure** (live, bulk-downloadable, cross-state lobbying disclosure data — LobbyView-for-states filling the 19-state gap OpenSecrets's 2022 scorecard acknowledges), with an N × 50 × 2 matrix (Required × {Legally Available, Practically Available}) as companion output. The field compendium unions 9+ source frameworks; the project **catalogs, does not grade**. No "Corda Rubric."

**Data model v1.1 shipped** (2026-04-22): generic `FrameworkReference` replacing hardcoded PRI/FOCAL IDs; availability axes + `EvidenceSource` on `FieldRequirement`; first-class `CompendiumItem` + `MatrixCell`; `ExtractionCapability` for per-state pipeline capability. The schema is the contract for both tracks below.

**Compendium populated + first multi-vintage-same-state SMR shipped (2026-04-30):** `statute-retrieval` merged to main with 118-row compendium, `cmd_build_smr` projection subcommand, and OH 2010 + OH 2025 SMRs (PRI-projection MVP). OH disclosure law structurally near-unchanged 2010 → 2025 as PRI projection sees it. Mid-session reframe: PRI's diagnostic yes/no rubric is *not* the right product shape for downstream filing extraction; the compendium-keyed `field_requirements` row IS, but the 22 fields populated by PRI projection are a thin slice of what the statute actually requires. Successor branches now split.

**Work splits into two tracks going forward:**

1. **Track A — Filing-schema-first extraction harness.** Replace the PRI-projection data source with direct statute extraction: required filings × required fields × cadence × triggers × cross-references. PRI/FOCAL/Sunlight become diagnostic *projections* of the schema, not data sources for it. The compendium-keyed `field_requirements` shape is preserved; only the data source changes. Successor branch to `statute-retrieval`; calibration anchor is the OH 2025 PRI-projection SMR (the new harness must be at least as comprehensive plus capture the dropped legal complexity).

2. **Track B — Pull disclosure data** (portal scraping + LLM extraction → `LobbyingFiling`). Unblocked by the OH SMR existing as a downstream contract. Other fellow has started this on `oh-portal-extraction` (not investigated this session). Portal snapshot corpus (981 artifacts, all 50 states) under `docs/historical/pri-2026-rescore/`.

**Fellow-to-track assignments pending** — Dan decides.

**Recently shipped (2026-04-22):** Fellow-facing landscape report at `docs/LANDSCAPE.md` (synthesis of 5 architectural pieces + 4-axis framing + positioning vs. OpenSecrets / Sunlight / LobbyView / CSG-Book-of-States, with Layer 1 diagram and verified OCDEP list). README "real-time" → "up-to-date" swap also landed (plus the equivalent swap in `CLAUDE.md` and the archived `pri_2026_methodology.md`) to match the framing set in `LANDSCAPE.md` §2.

Diff doc `docs/historical/statute-retrieval/results/20260430_oh_2025_vs_2010_diff.md`: structurally OH disclosure law is near-unchanged 2010 → 2025 as PRI projection sees it (one flip, `governors_office.required: False → True`; all field_requirements + reporting_parties + de_minimis nulls identical). Two known scorer blind spots flagged for the successor branch: qualitative materiality gates (§101.70(F) "main purposes" test) invisible to current scorer; conjunctive rubric items scored disjunctively under prompt drift. **The PRI-projection SMR is parking-orbit MVP** — the long-term shape (compendium-keyed `field_requirements`) is correct, but the data source (PRI yes/no diagnostic answers) is wrong for downstream filing extraction. - **2026-06-05** — [ny-disclosure-explore] **Phase 2 code-complete: `materialize_ny` + `materialize_cli` shipped, TDD.** Picked up the prior handoff (build the materialize step + thin CLI), honoring its "do not re-litigate the settled decisions" instruction. `io/ny/materialize.py` projects the collapsed filing grain into **4** `releases/ny/` TSVs shape-compatible with `releases/wi/`: `NY_clients` + `NY_lobbyists` (deduped Popolo `Organization`s), `NY_filings` (one row per (submission, client) at filing grain, comp carried once), `NY_filing_bill_links` (one row per (filing, real bill): even-split `comp_per_bill` + `filing_compensation` + `n_bills_in_filing` + `bill_print_version`). `io/ny/materialize_cli.py` wires the full Phase-2 pipeline over a raw Open NY bulk CSV (mirrors `io/wi/tier_2_materialize_cli.py`; adapted because NY reads raw CSV vs WI's checkpoints). **Even-split conserves dollars exactly under odd division** (integer-cent: `100/3 → 33.34+33.33+33.33 = 100.00`) — NY analog of WI's `modeled_hours_per_sponsor` invariant. Money stays Decimal end-to-end (materializer writes Decimal straight from grain, bypassing the `float`-typed model field). 11 new tests (RED confirmed before GREEN); NY-scoped suite 35 → **46 green locally**; ruff check + format clean. **Verification caveat:** full repo `pytest`/`ruff` not run — git push blocked in the agent env, worked via the GitHub REST API; verified GREEN locally against the real Pydantic models + Phase-0 fixture + CLI end-to-end. A networked `uv run pytest` run is owed. Commit `f1098b6b27`. Design choice flagged to Dan: filing split into two TSVs (grain + per-bill links), reversible. - **2026-06-05** — [ny-disclosure-explore] **Phase 2 grain-collapse guard + column map, TDD; amendment double-count finding.** Before building the load-bearing dollar-conservation guard, probed live `client_semiannual` and found the plan's dedup rule (*"keep latest `filing_type` per `form_submission_id`"*) is a **no-op** and the model is wrong: an amendment is a separate submission with its own id superseding the prior one, so naive distinct-`form_submission_id` comp summing double-counts every superseded version (**4.1× overcount** on `RIDDETT ASSOCIATES`/`TRIAL LAWYERS ASSN`: $255,536 filing booked as $1,050,542). A second probe confirmed `form_submission_id` is monotonic with submission order ⇒ verified fix = keep `max(form_submission_id)` per business key `(year, period, principal_lobbyist, beneficial_client, contractual_client_name)`. Logged on GH #37 (per Dan; now covers cross- *and* within-dataset double-count). Shipped TDD: `io/ny/grain.py` (`collapse_to_filing_grain`, 9 tests) + `io/ny/columns.py` (`normalize_columns`, 2 core datasets, 4 tests); probe scripts in `scripts/`. Code review caught a real NaN-business-key dollar-loss bug (groupby `dropna`), fixed + regression-tested. Full suite **1659 pass** + the 3 pre-existing #38 `scoring` reds (untouched). Convo: [`convos/20260605_ny_phase2_grain_collapse.md`](docs/active/ny-disclosure-explore/convos/20260605_ny_phase2_grain_collapse.md). **Next:** parser step — derive `bill_id` (resolve State-Bill/level scoping), parse to Pydantic, `materialize_ny` + CLI.

Both surface HTTP failures as typed errors, never a silent partial file/empty list. 10 green TDD tests; commit `db64354`, pushed. Reused WI's fetcher *flow* + `_FakeSession` mock boundary without cloning its HTML-scraper internals. Pre-code baseline = the established 1636-pass/3-fail; confirmed the 3 reds are the long-standing `scoring`-module data drift (CA snapshot pin `2026-04-13` vs only `2026-05-01` on disk) — now tracked as GH #38, left untouched per multi-committer hygiene. Two refinements surfaced — bill linkage is a typed subset, and the API is denormalized ~1,300× (client semi-annual 2025 = 11.2M rows but only 8,613 filings; comp replicated per row) ⇒ pull via bulk CSV, collapse to filing grain, never sum comp across raw rows. Decisions locked: chain spine = `client_semiannual` (`qym9-xzj6`); even-split per-bill dollars (`comp_per_bill` + raw filing comp + `n_bills_in_filing`, mirroring WI's `modeled_hours_per_sponsor` after Dan flagged the first "let consumer divide" framing as declining-to-model); strip `-A`/`-B` amendment suffix for the Open States join (web-confirmed = print version), preserve as `bill_print_version`, measure match both ways. Open States confirmed solid for NY; `parties_lobbied` skipped. Structural join smoke test against the 316 cached OH AER extractions in `data/oh_portal/extracted/`: **86.4% row-weighted match** (887 / 1,027 references) between extracted `positions[].bill_reference.bill_number` and `OH_136_bills.csv.identifier`. Top match HB 96 (state biennial budget, 81 references). The unmatched 13.6% are exclusively OAC administrative-rule citations (e.g., `5160-32-02`, `JC 4731-9-01`) — not bills, expected miss; future OH chain composer should classify these separately.

FTM site flagged as in sunset/integration mode pending OpenSecrets merger. Long-term API contract uncertain.

Documented bulk path is UI Transaction Search → CSV export, capped at 65K rows. **Selenium-driving the UI is the proven fallback:** IRW's `accountability_datacleaning` (open-source R + `campfin` package) successfully scraped old CFIS at 8.39M-record scale through 2023-06-04; their `ReceiptsList.csv` schema is 18 columns (TransactionDate, ContributorName/Address/Occupation/Employer, ContributionAmount, ContributorType, ReceivingCommitteeName, **ETHCFID** as stable recipient join key, Conduit, Branch, ...). **FollowTheMoney.org is the cheapest credible source:** 50-state coverage current through 2024, free academic/nonprofit API for Canary Institute, endpoint live-confirmed (`curl api.followthemoney.org` returns `{"error":"Invalid API Key"}` — gating, not absence). **No separate lobbyist personal-contribution filing in WI** — §13.625 imposes narrow temporal windows; lobbyist contributions land in CFIS as ordinary contribution rows, isolatable only via name-string match against the 773-entry lobbyist roster + `Occupation`/`EmployerName` disambiguation at $200+. **Recommendation:** **YES, cut a separate `wi-campaign-finance` implementation branch.** Phase 1 = 2-3-day FTM viability test against LeMahieu's SB 28 / 29-principal electric-utility coalition (the chain's cleanest signal); Phase 2 conditional on FTM GO = full ingest + chain join (3-5 days); Phase 3 conditional on FTM NO-GO = Selenium-Sunshine port reusing IRW's model (5-7 days). Added `num_sponsors_on_bill` + `modeled_hours_per_sponsor` (= modeled_hours / num_sponsors) so `SUM GROUP BY sponsor` stops inflating by sponsor count; `modeled_hours` preserved unchanged for backward-compat per-bill aggregations. Uniform-share is the v1.1 assumption flagged in the writeup; position-weighted attribution (lead author > 9th co-sponsor) is a v1.2 candidate. - **2026-05-31** — [wi-allocation-matrix] **Phase 2 — IPF fit + materialize + CLI landed; 27 new tests, zero regressions; first sharable allocation matrix on disk.** Empirical ipfn probe before tests verified the API at giant-CC scale (312L × 523P × 1,441E converges in ~340 ms hours_comm) AND surfaced two findings that revised the plan: (1) **Pettack is in her own 6L × 6P × 11E CC, not the giant** — the plan and Phase 1 results had assumed exclusion mechanics inside the giant CC fit, but her actual CC has internally-balanced marginals (730.2 hrs comm both sides, 3,454.5 vs 3,466.8 other), so IPF converges cleanly with no marginal surgery. The "exclusion" collapses from a fit-modification question to a labeling question. (2) **Bipartite support encoded via seed = 1.0 on edges, 0.0 elsewhere** — ipfn preserves zeros at machine precision (max leak = 0.0), no explicit constraint handling needed. Confidence-label schema negotiated with Dan: `exact` (singleton CC), `ipf_fit` (free CC, nonzero marginal, not flagged), `zero_filed` (lobbyist filed 0 hrs — DELIBERATELY preserved per Dan's call so downstream consumers can spot "principal paid, lobbyist filed 0" patterns), `aggregation_flagged` (Pettack-class). Pushback exchange with Dan on Pettack labeling: avoided "illegal" framing without WI §13.62 evidence; landed on descriptive `aggregation_flagged`. Phase 2 also added `min_hours_for_ratio_flag` param to `flag_outliers` (Phase 1 follow-up, live default 10.0 in `fit_all` — suppresses the 4 H1/H2 tiny-hours false positives). Surfaced a Phase 3+ candidate: **small-CC over-attribution pattern** — 8 H1 CCs have principal-side hours exceeding the sum of their authorized lobbyists' filed hours, IPF assigns all of it to the one filing lobbyist (CC #4: 19.9 prin hrs / 14 lob hrs → 42% over-attribution). Mathematically correct but invisible in the `ipf_fit` label; flagged but not fixed this phase. **Materialized output:** `data/allocations/WI/WI_lobbyist_principal_hours_h{1,2}_2025.tsv` with schema `(lobbyist_id, principal_id, hours_comm, hours_other, confidence)` — 1,912 H1 / 2,055 H2 rows. H1 confidence dist: 6.4% `exact` / 90.8% `ipf_fit` / 2.5% `zero_filed` / 0.3% `aggregation_flagged`. Hand-spot-check 4 cells: DoorDash principal-col-sum EXACTLY matches source (83.90 == 83.9, 87.50 == 87.5). 5 commits this session (`945cefb` `min_hours` param, `6a6ae63` RED IPF, `5bfb476` GREEN IPF, `fc08b2a` materialize, `03702b8` CLI + stdout cleanup). Full suite at **1,610 passed** + same 3 pre-existing `test_pipeline.py` baseline failures. - **2026-05-30 (afternoon → evening)** — [wi-allocation-matrix] **Executed Phase 0 (audit, no code) + Phase 1 (TDD: loaders + bipartite graph + CC decomposition + outlier flagging).** Phase 0 surfaced 4 findings reshaping the plan: lobbyist filings are semester-granular (release README mislabels as quarterly — source code at `tier_2_materialize.py:12` is correct), moots the convo's "time-granularity mismatch" framing; percent-rounding is structural (only 41% of 1,428 (principal, period) groups sum to 100%, max 100% — asymmetric undercounting that adds Q6 for Phase 3 boundary); active edges per semester ~1,912 H1 / 2,055 H2 (not 2,254 biennium-union); one giant 835-node CC dominates H1 (900-node in H2) — confirms the plan's "What could change" worst case. Direct fixture inspection: the WI portal renders each contact field as `<i class="fa-{phone,envelope,globe}"></i> {value}<br/>` where value is a NavigableString *sibling* of the icon (not a descendant); both parsers' `_extract_address` were over-collecting those sibling strings. Re-materialized full corpus (18.9 s; identical 944/773/1706/3092/7345/0 row counts; addresses verified clean across 6 spot-check rows; one preserved portal-side artifact — lobbyist 11308 still shows `"Madison, WI 53703, WI 53703"` from the source HTML). - **2026-05-14 (research arc doc)** — [extraction-harness-brainstorm + main] **Three-prong project arc + Prong 1 internals + Ralph loop doc landed on main.** Convo [`20260514_research_arc_doc.md`](docs/active/extraction-harness-brainstorm/convos/20260514_research_arc_doc.md). New repo-level doc [`docs/RESEARCH_ARC.md`](docs/RESEARCH_ARC.md) (213 lines, 2 mermaid diagrams). Started as branch-status query, evolved into research-arc review. **Three user-initiated reframes of the agent's mental model:** (1) Phase C is the Ralph-loop evaluation function, not a downstream sanity-check — no 50-state typed-cell ground truth exists, so projecting cells back into each rubric's published scoring is the only tractable accuracy signal for Prong 1; (2) Prong 2 + Prong 3 is the product, Prong 1 is upstream scaffolding (SMR encodes what each state's regime *legally requires*; gap vs. what portals *actually expose* is itself a research artifact; shared typed-cell schema makes Prong 2 dramatically cheaper); (3) Locked rubric order is mostly convenience, not a rigid signal-strength gradient (respects dependencies, starts where ground truth is strongest, but reordering on empirical results is fine). **Ralph-loop concretized:** objective `loss(prompt) = Σ over (state, vintage, rubric) |f_rubric(SMR) − published_score|`; single scalar. Noise floor `σ_noise` from independent re-runs is prerequisite (otherwise loop chases 1σ flukes; distinct from Track A's across-vintage stability check). Three risks named: implicit weighting in `Σ |diff|`; Goodhart on projection-distance (rubrics have degenerate solutions, Track A's across-vintage stability is the only out-of-distribution check); cost asymmetry (single-state OH-only ≈ $4/iter vs 50-state ≈ $150/iter under $50–500/mo budget). **Cross-track milestone surfaced:** first Ralph-loop iteration end-to-end needs `scoring_v2` (this branch) + CPI 2015 C11 projection (`phase-c-projection-tdd`) + OH 2015 statute bundle (`oh-statute-retrieval`) converging across three branches. Walks back agent's earlier overclaim that "harness feature-complete after scoring_v2" — that's extraction-complete, not quality-validated. **Cherry-pick path executed cleanly:** pulled 2 unseen commits on origin/extraction-harness-brainstorm (`5f262e9` + `7d6f20d` — desktop T1 + fixture decouple) → committed doc as `ee75e3a` → main worktree fast-forwarded mid-session by `compendium-naming-docs` PR #10 merge → cherry-picked clean as `86dc02e` → pushed both branches. **README.md discoverability link deferred per user.** No code changes; no extraction-harness component movement; no plan revisions.
